ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u68	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I submit a report to the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications of the Government draft law amending the Code of Conduct in Criminal Matters, print No. 1562. Marshal of the Sejm at the 43rd meeting of the Sejm on 7 June 2017 pursuant to Article. 87. 2 in conjunction with Article. 90. 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Extraordinary Commission for amendments to the codifications for its consideration. The Commission, after considering this proposal at its meeting on 20 June 2017, asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. I would like to remind you that the project concerns the introduction of facilities for the payment of a fine imposed by means of a criminal mandate by introducing a non-cash form and using a payment card or another payment instrument for this purpose. During the committee's work, an amendment was made to the article. 2 extending the period of vacathio legis from 14 days to 30 days from the date of announcement. This extension aims, on the one hand, at better technical preparation for the implementation of the provisions of this law by various types of public services applying criminal mandates in criminal proceedings and, on the other hand, at the introduction of billing authorization devices. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u70	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present the position of the club. The draft law amends the Code of Conduct in cases of misconduct by introducing the possibility of payment of a fine imposed by a criminal mandate in a non-cash form by means of a payment card or other payment instrument. This is another form next to a cash, credit and default mandate. This regulation is particularly important for temporary residents in Poland, who so far are obliged to pay the fine specified in the criminal mandate in the form of cash to the officer who issued the mandate. At the same time, persons who are permanently resident in the territory of the Republic of Poland or who are permanently resident or resident in the territory of the Member States of the European Union who, in their current legal status, can only receive a credit mandate, which requires payment within 7 days of receipt. The officer is required to inform the punished person of the possibility of payment of the fine in a non-cash form and of the costs incurred by the punished person in connection with the authorisation of the transaction and the transfer of funds to the relevant bank account. Therefore, the principle of freedom in the selection of a fine by the punished person in a non-cash form applies. Up to now, only inspectors of Road Transport Inspections in the Polish legal system can collect the required deposit in non-cash form by means of a payment card pursuant to Article. Article 94 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 5 of the Road Transport Act of 6 September 2001. This solution will allow the application of a non-cash criminal mandate by several services, inspections and guards. High Seym! The Law and Justice Club strongly supports this good, practical bill. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u92	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present a position vis-à-vis the Government's draft law amending the Act on the Protection of Employee Claims in the event of the insolvency of the employer and the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters, Print No 1605, introducing legislation aimed at extending the protection of claims of employees who, in the event of insolvency of the employer, have been deprived of employment and the benefits they are entitled to, as well as speeding up the procedure for granting financial assistance to employees in connection with the actual cessation of their activities. An extension of the protection of employees' claims will be achieved by: firstly, extending the reference periods between the termination of the employment relationship and the date of the employer's insolvency from 9 to 12 months, which will allow the protection of employees' claims whose entitlement to benefit was not within the current reference periods, i.e. in 9 months, in view of the relatively long waiting period for judgments of the labour court and the settlement of the economic court in respect of the application for bankruptcy, often preventing the maintenance of the current reference period, which is 9 months between the termination of the employment relationship and the date of the insolvency of the employer; secondly, the proposed solution enabling the court to dismiss an application for bankruptcy where the employer's assets are not sufficient to meet the costs of proceedings or are sufficient to meet the costs of proceedings, the payment of benefits, including where the termination of the employment relationship does not take place for a period of not more than 4 months following the date of the insolvency of the employer; thirdly, the extension of the catalogue of payment of unmet employees' claims for the equivalent of unused leave for the year preceding the year in which the employment relationship ceased - according to the new proposal, the payment of benefits from the equivalent of unused leave will also be possible if the termination of the employment relationship occurred within a period of not more than 4 months after the date of insolvency of the employer; fourthly, the extension of the definition of the worker, because the proposed amendment provides that the protection of the employer's claims will also be covered by such persons employed as a spouse, by the employer, by the spouse, by the parents of the spouse who are also having the child who are excluded. In turn, the acceleration of the procedure for granting financial assistance to employees will be achieved by: a more precise definition of the concept of actual cessation of activities by the employer, thus specifying the cases in which the advance payments to future employee benefits can be made quickly; a deformity of the procedure for payment of advances - as the advance is intended to be immediate aid, the presence of the Marshal of voivodeship to the employee of providing further documents and setting deadlines for their delivery has been waived; a clarification that the basis for payment of benefits will be the average monthly remuneration in force on the date of submission of the list or application for payment of benefits. The previous records were vague, as they stated that this was to be the average monthly remuneration of the previous quarter, from the date of its announcement by the President of the CSO. With such a provision, there have been cases of withholding benefits until the publication of an appropriate average. The amendment proposed will reduce the waiting time for benefits. Another positive amendment proposed in the proposed bill is the exemption from the relative charges in labour law cases concerning the recovery of claims arising from the implementation of the provisions on the protection of employees' claims in the event of the insolvency of the employer, where the value of the subject matter of the dispute exceeds 50 000. zł. The proposed solution will allow for a more rational management of the funds of the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund, which should be used primarily for the payment of benefits to employees, rather than for the payment of judicial costs in the course of recovery proceedings against insolvent employers. The proposed amendments, which are of a more precise nature, clarify certain provisions, will eliminate cases of ambiguities. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the Government bill amending the Act on the Protection of Employee Claims in the event of the insolvency of the employer and the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u93	Thank you very much, Member. I invite Mrs Izabela Katarzyna Mrzygłocka, Civic Platform.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u94	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present an opinion on the report of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the governmental draft law amending the Act on the Protection of Employee Claims in the event of the insolvency of the employer and the Act on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters. The project is included in print No 1605 and the report - in sejm printing No 1640. The proposed changes are the result of an ongoing analysis of the implementation of tasks resulting from the Act of 13 July 2006 on the Protection of Employee Claims in the event of the insolvency of the employer. The report on the implementation of the Fund's financial plan for Guaranteed Occupational Benefits shows that in 2015 the benefits were paid out to 19 000. employees in connection with the insolvency of 889 employers. The draft law introduces regulations to speed up the provision of financial assistance to workers who, in the event of insolvency of their employer, have been deprived of their employment and their employment benefits. The aim of the proposed amendment is also to ensure more effective protection of employees' claims, among others. by extending the withdrawal catalogue, extending from 9 to 12 months the reference periods between the termination of the employment relationship and the date of the employer's insolvency. The practice shows that workers' benefits have often been refused because of exceeding the reference periods in force so far. The proposed amendments will allow the payment of the benefit if the employment relationship has ceased within no more than 4 months after the date of the employer's insolvency. This includes the equivalent for vacation leave, but also remuneration if the court dismisses an application for bankruptcy. The proposed amendment also provides for an extension of the definition of worker in Article. Ten bills. Currently, the article deprives the status of an employee of a person associated with the employer's relationship with marriage, kinship or affinity. The proposed wording aims to provide workers with protection. This means that the protection of employees' claims will also be covered, inter alia. those employed by the employer, such as the spouse of the employer and their own children, who have so far been excluded from this protection. In view of the inconsistent case law of the general courts, the proposal provides for a revision of the Article. 35 the Law of 28 July 2005 on judicial costs in civil matters. The amendment consists of the exemption from a comparative fee from labour law actions relating to the recovery of claims by the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will vote in favour of the draft report in print No 1640, with amendments tabled by the Legislative Bureau. Thank you very much.

It should also be added that such a solution applies to diplomatic and military institutions located outside the country. As we read in the explanatory memorandum, the purpose of the law itself is to focus pro-export and pro-investment tasks in one institution operating on a one-stop shop. The Agency will operate within the Polish Development Fund group, which will ensure efficient cooperation of the agencies with other development institutions subordinate to the Minister for Development and Finance, but more about this in the further part of the speech. The proposed solutions are also aimed at improving coordination of the promotion of the Polish economy abroad by replacing the inefficient trade and investment promotion departments of embassies and consulates of the Republic of Poland directly subordinate to the minister responsible for economic affairs into a network of new foreign trading offices of subordinate agencies. The need for change and the coordination of action itself is also very clear in the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber, which was published in the fourth quarter of the month. This analysis covered the years 2010 to 2015. You can find information there, among others. that in these years there was no separate national strategy for economic promotion of Poland. A single definition of such promotion has not been adopted in the current legislation. The promotional activities of the public entities were not coordinated. The attempts made by the Minister responsible for the economy in this regard have been ineffective. The Polish Council of Promotion appointed by the President of the Council of Ministers under the chairmanship of the Minister of Foreign Affairs did not fulfil its role. In practice, the Council did not take action to promote our country's economy. It did not make recommendations to the Council of Ministers and did not propose or give an opinion on legislative solutions in this regard. Between 2010 and 2015, the Council met 16 times and adopted seven resolutions, including only three substantive resolutions. I think it sums up in an excellent way how Poland's economic promotion was conducted in previous years. The bill discussed today meets the expectations of the Supreme Audit Chamber and Polish entrepreneurs. The Agency will become a point of first contact for the exporter and the investor and will be a knowledge centre for the entrepreneur planning the development of investment or export activities, and the Agency will use a network of new foreign trading offices in its activities. The offer of PAIiH for entrepreneurs is intended to include a common system of promotion of Polish companies and goods abroad, analytical support, co-operation consulting, localization consulting and acting as an investor spokesman. The Polish Investment and Trade Agency alongside the Export Credit Insurance Corporation, Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, the Polish Development Fund, the Industrial Development Agency and the Polish Enterprise Development Agency will be part of the Polish Development Fund group. In this context, PAIiH will not only offer its advisory and promotion services, but also promote among its clients the instruments of other PFR institutions, such as R &amp; D funding at home and abroad, risk reduction, support for the innovation system in order to prepare a comprehensive offer for the business. PAIiH will also, in close cooperation with the Minister responsible for the economy, mediate in offering state support instruments to the investor. High Izbo! The joint committees took note of the draft law on 20 June and request the adoption of the draft and the solutions proposed therein, together with the amendments adopted by the committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u136	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! I will give you detailed answers to these questions in writing, but I would point out the following matters on an ongoing basis. There are a lot of changes, and we want to change that so that the administration is for the market and not the market is for the administration. It's time we understood this big change. Such concepts as project management and management by objectives or matrix management, or, as Mr KPIs, key performance indicators mentioned here, are practically unknown in the administration. These are the things we are implementing that will enable us to manage this whole process concerning foreign branches of PAIiH. Another big change is that as of today 70% export from Poland is to the countries of the European Union, where we have a very similar right, we have access to the Internet, we can practically, in an hour, with two planes for this small money to reach there and meet with the contractor and find out how to do business there. We now want to enter new foreign markets, where the economy is often growing almost and double-digitly every year, and in the EU we have 1% every year, and promote the Polish economy in those distant markets. Small businesses don't want it, there's too much cost to find out what the work culture is there, what the rights are, find contractors. That is why we want to eliminate our or most of our trade and investment departments in Europe and open them to new prospective markets in Asia and South Africa, South America. As for the staff here, we're hoping there's gonna be a big change. As of today, WPHI staff come from Poland. I was personally in the United States a few months ago, where a person who worked in WPHI, who had been there for several years, did not speak a word in English. It's kind of hard to do business if you don't even know the language. So the intention here is that most people who will be employed will be employed in local markets, where they will work, where they have already shown that they have very good discernment, know people, know topics and have already done something in business life that they can carry on. As far as costs are concerned, we expect that, compared to those costs that are currently at what we have already opened -- I think we have 12 -- there will be 3 times lower costs. This is due to the fact that we pay for local offices and employ locally, not as it is today, that we have to send all the guards from Poland, so even the accountant of such offices leaves Poland and we have to pay for the whole thing: for water to pay, for the apartment to pay, for the flight there, and it does not bring anything to the Polish economy. So the costs, as we expected, are three times lower, the number of people employed for the same reason - for today we employ about 300 people - we expect, will ultimately be in the new PAIiH abroad 150 people, half less. And again, these people will be just to do business, not to support this office locally. As regards the division of competences between the Ministry of Economic Development and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs is from doing economic policy. This is to talk to the local administration of a given country to sign bilateral agreements, not to support a particular entrepreneur. As for the Action Plan, the Action Plan is also as much as possible part of the whole Responsible Development Plan, and here we have which sectors we will promote, in which countries we will exist. And I think that's pretty much it. Thank you very much.

The committee's report is in print No 1641. Sejm at its 44th meeting on 22 June 2017 pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy for consideration. The proposed law is the implementation of the proposals and recommendations contained in the review of family support schemes, where there are proposals for changes in the area of education, family benefits, alimony collection, care for children up to the age of 3 and the Charter of the Large Family. The amendments concern the following laws: the Law on Family Benefits, the Law on Assistance to People with Child Support, the Law on State Aid to Child Education, the Law on Child Welfare under the age of 3, the Act on the Charter of the Large Family. The main assumptions of the groundbreaking family policy programme remain unchanged. The proposed law is intended to eliminate a few, albeit undesirable phenomena. The amendments consist in introducing the above mentioned amendments. the law excluding the application of the income loss mechanism in the event of loss of employment or other economic work, including non-agricultural economic activities, by the person concerned and the re-employment of that person in a short period of time with the same employer, payer or contracting entity or the re-starting of non-agricultural economic activity; the determination of the income of non-agricultural economic activities subject to taxation on the basis of provisions with flat-rate income tax on certain income generated by natural persons; the provisions concerning the conditions for the granting of education to persons declaring to raise their children alone; the organisation of the treatment of cases relating to Union coordination of social security systems; the way of determining the model of the application for the provision of education and annexes to the application; the extension of the period for the submission of applications for the provision of education for a further period ofeducation , their processing and payment of the benefitsgranted ; the establishment of the right to provide education to children who are dependent on parents who do not form afamily ; the catalogue of circumstances considered to be loss and obtainincome ; the scope of the data contained in the application for the provision ofeducation ; the performance of information to the competent authority of the Polishfamily. As regards the amendments to the Family Benefits Act, they concern the cancellation of the planned increase from 1 November 2017 in the income criteria for entitlement to family benefits and the increase in family benefits; the addition of a catalogue of income not subject to tax on natural persons, consisting of income on the basis of which the right to income dependent on family benefits is determined, for grants granted by the European Union to unemployed persons; the determination of the income of non-agricultural economic activities subject to taxation on the basis of provisions on flat-rate income tax on certain income generated by natural persons; clarification of Article. 6 of the Act by indicating that the family allowance for persons over the age of 18 is payable until the end of the academic year in which the end of the higher education course was completed, no later than the end of the 24th year of age; clarification of the article. Article 10 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the Act by indicating that the care of the child during the period of parental leave is entitled to one supplement to the family allowance regardless of the number of children for which parental leave has been granted; changes to the article. Article 15b (1) (a) (ii) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 4 by indicating that one-off child benefit is not entitled to a child's birth benefit where a single parent has not been established on the basis of an implementing title from the court or approved by a court for the benefit of the child from the other parent; clarification of the article. Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 5 A law aimed at ensuring uniform application of the provisions of the Act by the competent authorities and unambiguously indicating that it is not possible to accumulate many types of benefits financed from the budget of the State which are linked to the lack of professional activity caused by custody of the child or another member of the family; clarification of the article. 16a and Article 16a Article 17 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 6 Act by adding the provision that the status of job seeker or unemployed person does not affect the right of guardian of a disabled person to a special care allowance or care provision. The proposed amendments to the law on assistance to persons entitled to maintenance are specific amendments which are similar to the proposed amendments to the provisions of the Law on State aid to raise children and the Law on Family Benefits. Changes in the Children's Care Act under the age of 3 are among others:: extension of the list of entities that can start a nursery or a children's club; optimization of the requirements of the premises at which the nursery is to be run; changes in the qualifications of staff employed in a nursery or a children's club; clarification of the rules on food in nursery and children's clubs; changes in the register of nursery and children's clubs; preferences for multi-child families and children with disabilities when they are admitted to nursery and children's clubs; changes in the scope of the obligation for crèches to make monthly payments to PFRON; changes in reporting. As regards amendments to the Large Family Charter Act, these include:: visualization of the Large Family Card on mobile devices; extension of the circle of persons entitled to hold a card for parents whose children no longer fulfil the conditions for granting the card; change in the period of validity of the card for a full-time child studying at higher school; extension of the circle of foreigners having the right to a card; creation of a record by an entity selected by the Minister responsible for family affairs of the electronic system providing the card in the form of an electronic document. In the course of the examination of the draft, the Commission accepted amendments concerning, inter alia,: no need to introduce exemptions from agricultural and forestry tax in the case of crèches; clarification of the determination of the right to benefits on the basis of the monthly income of the family; clarification of the data to be provided in the certificate by the head of the tax office so that the income of clearing persons can be determined on the basis of the provisions on flat-rate income tax on certain income generated by natural persons; introduction of a three-month period during which single persons can present an implementing title, which shows that from the second parent they are entitled to child support without losing their right to benefits for that period; a solution enabling parents' advice in the nursery or children's club to collect voluntary contributions from parents or to raise funds from other sources and to collect these funds in a separate bank account; changes related to the need to eliminate data on registered children at the stage of applying for entry in the register of crèches and children's clubs; clarification of the question of keeping in force of activities carried out by the voivodship; failure to attach to the application for the granting of the Large Family Card documents which the municipality can obtain itself. The Commission shall act in accordance with Article. Article 43 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 At the same time, at the request of the applicants, the Sejm shall submit seven minority proposals concerning, inter alia,: transfer of coordination from the Marshal's office to the social security system in the voivodeship; extension to 25 years of age to which the family allowance is payable; extension of the catalogue of persons entitled to preferential admission to crèche, to children of single parents; grant an allowance of PLN 400 for each child under the care of a parent on parental leave. The Social Policy and Family Committee, after considering the draft Print Act No 1625 at its meeting on 22 June 2017, asks the High Sejm to approve the above draft Law. On behalf of the committee, I request that the report submitted in print No 1641 be adopted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

In order to effectively prevent the matching of income, the application of the provisions on loss of income in the event of loss of employment and the re-employment of that person in the same employer is excluded. In the case of flat-rate business income tax, monthly income will be the amount announced by the Minister responsible for Family Affairs. The child benefit to a single parent will be granted only after the possibility of obtaining maintenance benefits from the second parent has been exhausted. Due to numerous complaints from citizens who are waiting for the decision of the Marshals of voivodships for a few months, as well as a long delay in implementation, the proposed rules are intended to ensure better organisation and faster processing of applications. Any dispute between the voivodship and the competent authority will be settled by the Supreme Administrative Court and complaints against the activities of the voivodship will be dealt with by the competent minister. In order to speed up the process of applying for a model application and annexes, it will be published on the Ministry's BIP website. In order to avoid queues, an extension of applications is introduced: applications for education and maintenance from the maintenance fund - from 1 August 2017, applications for family allowance and care allowance - from 1 September 2017. The provision on the amount of parental benefit in the case of alternating childcare and the circumstances of loss of income and the scope of data in the application shall be clarified. A solution is proposed to eliminate the cases of receiving benefits by people who do not live in Poland. The third stage of the verification of applications is being removed. The income catalogue for unemployed or receiving scholarships shall be supplemented. The following changes facilitate the establishment of childcare facilities for up to 3 years. This will increase the number of childcare places and at the same time allow parents to combine their professional and family responsibilities. In turn, the development of childcare institutions for up to 3 years will increase employment, so support will be directed to local governments with high unemployment. High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u178	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury on the Government's draft law amending the Law on oil stocks, petroleum products and natural gas and on the principles of dealing with situations of threat to the fuel safety of the state and disturbances on the oil market and certain other laws, prints No 1630 and 1669. The practice indicates that there is a need to clarify the provisions of the Energy Law introduced on 22 July 2016. First of all, the question of interpretation must be overcome by introducing new provisions. The new regulations are intended to protect Poland in a realistic way in the event of an energy crisis, mainly by establishing new rules for maintaining mandatory stocks, introducing transparent rules on the mobilisation of mandatory natural gas stocks, adapting legislation to current practice, reducing certain administrative obligations, defining the regulatory competence of entities responsible for maintaining mandatory natural gas stocks and setting out transparent rules for clearing costs arising from the mobilisation of mandatory natural gas stocks. Changes are necessary for the country's energy security in the event of an unforeseen crisis. The proposed solution assumes that for stocks of mandatory natural gas held outside Poland it will be necessary to reserve continuous capacity only for the purpose of bringing all stocks into the national gas system within a period of not more than 40 days. The changes will make it possible to introduce additional quantities of natural gas into the national system at any time above the contracted quantities. Such a solution is necessary because capacity cannot be used by an entity obliged to maintain mandatory stocks for the purpose of current trading, as only the priority of transmission would change in the event of a crisis. Instead of trading volumes, compulsory stocks would be sent first. The amount of fuel entering the country would not change, because in the event of a crisis, capacity would still be likely to be used entirely for the transmission of commercial volumes. The simplest thing is that fuel would be outside the borders of Poland, but it could not be introduced into the national system so quickly. Had it not been for the provision of continuous capacity only and exclusively for the import of compulsory stocks, the very idea of stockpiling would not have been necessary at all, as transmission networks would not be able to transfer both commercial volumes and mandatory stocks at the same time. In the event of a crisis, the transport of mandatory stocks would be at the expense of commercial volumes. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! We consider my club to be very good, but we want to present and place on the hands of Mr Marshal nine amendments, which are both substantive and precise. 1. The aim of the amendment is to define the content of the contracts by defining the purpose rather than the precise content of the contracts. 2. amendment, amendment to the wording of the article. 24b. The aim is to ensure the provision of the so-called "supplies'. Ticket services only by professional operators, i.e. undertakings licensed to trade in natural gas with foreign countries or to trade in gaseous fuels. In addition, this amendment with regard to the proposed new wording aims to reduce the regulatory burden on entities wishing to commission mandatory stockholding tasks. Amendment 3 consists in supplementing Article. Paragraph 25 is replaced by the following: 11, which is the basis for the President of the URE to obtain information, in particular contact details concerning natural gas importing entities. Amendment 4 - The aim is to provide tools for effective implementation of monitoring and control tasks and sanctions against the President of the URE [[♪ The bell ♪]] entities carrying out maintenance tasks for mandatory stocks of natural gas. The rest of the amendments will be discussed together at an additional meeting of the Committee on Energy and the Treasury.

It is worrying that the law, which will have a huge impact on the shaping of the fuel market in Poland, has not been consulted with market operators. The Ministry of Energy, when drawing up the project, did not submit it for opinion either to the President of the Energy Regulatory Office or to the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection. Opinions were only created after the project was transferred to the Sejm. The Government of the Civic Platform and PSL started liberalisation of the gas market since 2013. We then introduced exemptions from gas storage for small companies, gas prices lower than those set by the URE, the gas exchange. Thanks to these treatments, the monopoly position of PGNiG has decreased, companies trading up to 100 million m3 of gas per year have entered the market intensively, competitiveness has increased and the gas market has been diversified. The most important thing for the PO-PSL government was that the market would grow and the price of gas would fall. The recordings of the project in its current shape will stop the liberalisation of the gas market, lead to a significant increase in gas prices and will negatively affect the competitiveness of the Polish economy. The changes in the gas storage obligation adopted in September 2016 will enter into force from October 2016, but already now, due to the long-term nature of gas purchase contracts, the price increase was almost immediately and reached between 4 and 5%. The 2016 changes led to a situation in which gas prices on the Polish stock exchange are on average 9 PLN for 1 MWh higher than on the German stock exchange. Currently, the projector imposes an additional obligation in the case of gas storage abroad, namely the obligation to maintain capacity booking at entry and exit points in the event of a crisis. In addition, the additional capacity paid by the trader on the interconnector will only be used at a time of crisis. In both the opinion of the OMC and the Council on Gas Confederation Leviathan this solution does not make sense and will be completely cost-effective. It will lead to a further 5% increase in gas prices which will hit the final customer, and in the worst case scenario will lead to the cessation of gas imports by companies and the termination of their activities. The President of the URE also critically refers to the maintenance of capacity exclusively for mandatory stocks. In his opinion, these provisions are incompatible with European Union law, inter alia. This puts PGNiG in the role of a monopoly of storage services for the gas market, which in turn raises concerns of UOKiK about the possibility of fraud. The President of the UOKiK points out that the proposed changes put PGNiG as the only entity that will not be affected by the new loads and will even benefit from them. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u188	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I have the pleasure to present the position of the Poselski Club Modern in relation to the government's draft law amending the Law on oil stocks, oil products and natural gas and the principles of dealing with situations of threat to the fuel security of the state and disturbances on the oil market and certain other laws. These changes consisted in removing the existing preference for the sale of natural gas with a supply at the border by imposing an obligation to maintain mandatory stocks of natural gas on the operators making these imports for their own use. More importantly, this law also removed the possibility of obtaining exemption from this obligation so-called. Little salesmen. These regulations, I'll remind you, aroused understandable controversy at the time. The pace of legislative work carried out last year was very high, and the effect is today in the form of many errors or shortcomings that emerged in those regulations. This novel is an attempt to remove some of these mistakes made last year. The Act introduces in particular, we see this scope, several facilitations concerning the obligation to hold mandatory stocks of natural gas, and in particular reduces administrative obligations by abolishing the need to present to the transmission system operator or system operator the characteristics of the storage facility located in the territory of the Republic of Poland where the stocks are held. According to this change, this obligation will be imposed on the operator of the storage system. The draft amendment confirms the practice so far that the quantity of stocks is determined on the basis of net imports of natural gas, i.e. gas which reaches customers in the territory of the Republic of Poland, not gross imports. The amendments prejudice that for stocks of mandatory natural gas held outside the territory of the Republic of Poland it will be necessary to reserve continuous capacity. This is the element in this bill, which is probably the most controversial. I understand the desire to ensure 100% that these stocks, which will be abroad, will actually be available at a time of crisis in the country, I understand that very well, it was probably the main element that decided on these regulations, but it must be remembered that our gas system is not yet perfect and the gas capacity at the border is limited. Nor do we have as many warehouses in the country as we would like. If there were more of these warehouses, especially if they were built not only by PGNiG, but also by other companies, there would be sufficient competition here, which in any case is needed. Hence, there is a legitimate concern that these regulations may result in price increases, because operators will, of course, try these additional costs, and maintaining continuous capacity is a high cost, pass it on to final customers. Whether they will be able to do so or whether PGNiG will prevent them from doing so - I think no one knows the answer to this question yet. We have read the details of URE and UOKiK. On the other hand, the government's policy continues to strengthen the PGNiG monopoly. This monopoly is still present, as it is a state-owned company, it has a monopoly and has instruments to regulate it. These first records, which were earlier that we took down, about the fact that small entities do not need to maintain reserves, resulted in the fact that the large entity, PGNiG, is a monopoly and has opportunities. It was just about giving a better chance, an opportunity to even up economic opportunities, and right now it's been taken off. From what the suppliers present in their comments made to the URE, it appears that they will lose their ability to compete completely with this supplier, i.e. they will fall out of the market, in other words. If they fall off the market, there's only one supplier left. The question is, what will be the role and purpose of maintaining the gas exchange, who will compete with whom when there is one supplier, how it will translate into gas prices. Gas prices for industry, for the economy, for industrial enterprises will be freed from autumn, so this will affect our economy as a whole - let's not just look at individual consumers. This raises our serious concerns. In recent years, there has been a steady price of gas, little of it, for the last eight years, as someone would have said, for eight years there has been a fall in gas prices for individual customers. Competitively, we were able to do this. At the moment, gas prices are starting to rise. Our concerns are quite high in this respect, and I think that, with such a sensitive subject as energy security, how to maintain gas supplies for our economy and for individual consumers, it should be very reasonable and prudent to approach all these solutions, because it is a very sensitive subject and even if there are any comments on the existing functioning of this market, [[♪ The bell ♪]] this needs to be analysed very thoroughly and taken into account all the comments, and these comments, as you can see, are very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u191	Thank you very much. Mr Ireneusz Gaina, near Free and Solidarnia, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u192	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the draft law amending the Law on oil stocks, oil products and natural gas and the rules for dealing with situations of threat to the fuel security of the state and disturbances on the oil market and certain other laws. The bill submitted concerns the basic tasks of the State in the area of security of natural gas supply and brings Poland closer to the objective of building the position of a gas hub country in Central and Eastern Europe. The Act amending the Energy Law and certain other laws adopted on 22 July 2016 introduced new solutions to improve security of supply of natural gas. They consisted in removing the existing preference of the natural gas seller with the supply at the border by imposing an obligation to maintain mandatory stocks of natural gas on operators importing natural gas for their own use and removing the possibility of obtaining exemption from that obligation for so-called. Little salesmen. An analysis of the experience with the application of the new provisions on mandatory natural gas stocks shows that legislative action is needed to facilitate the maintenance of mandatory natural gas stocks by operators and to resolve the interpretation doubts raised by market participants in relation to previous changes. In the light of the proposed provisions, it would be beneficial for the entities mentioned in the Act to transfer administrative obligations to operators of the storage system related to the need to present the characteristics of the storage facility located in the territory of the Republic of Poland and to introduce transparent rules for the mobilisation of mandatory stocks of natural gas by the gas transmission system operator. The formula for establishing transparent and non-discriminatory clearing rules for costs arising from the mobilisation of mandatory stocks of natural gas on the basis of the market price will improve the competitiveness of the gas market. In addition, the definition of rules for the impact of the market regulator on those entrusted with maintaining mandatory stocks of natural gas and the determination of clear interpretative doubts regarding the rules governing the functioning of the security of gas supply system will reduce the regulatory risks associated with the establishment of economic activities in this area. The comments submitted by the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection in a letter dated 30 June 2012 should be considered relevant for the further legislative work. They relate in particular to the monopolistic position of the subsidiary of PGNiG - System Operator Warehouse Sp. z o.o. He is the only entrepreneur to whom other obliged entities can commission a service to maintain mandatory reserves in Poland on their behalf. This creates a disadvantage for these entities, in which they will have to compete with PGNiG, which has control over the company that operates the ticket service for them, which may give rise to a legitimate concern about the potential breach of public competition rules by abuse of a dominant position by the PGNiG group company. In a similar tone, the President of the Energy Regulatory Office, who, by submitting a number of important comments in his letter of 29 June this year, pointed to an increase of PLN 3.46/MWh in the cost of gas acquisition by companies holding stocks abroad. The consequence of this is the consolidation of the PGNiG monopoly with the need for administrative regulation, which makes the gas market in Poland less efficient. During the discussions at the Energy and Treasury Commission meeting, there was a problem of physical storage and transmission of gas from abroad. Indeed, in order to ensure that no matter what the circumstances, access to gas will always be possible, which will be stored outside Poland, interconnectors and transmission networks must have adequate capacity. The creation of high-capacity interconnectors will also enable the proper functioning of the European Union SoS regulation favourable to Poland, which will enter into force on 1 October this year. It is also necessary to increase the number of smaller warehouses on the territory of the Republic of Poland, which will positively increase our energy security. The scope of the necessary infrastructure investment should therefore be coordinated with the obligations imposed by the law. The Committee on Energy and the Treasury has adopted several substantive and editorial amendments, which find our acceptance. Mr Marshal, High Izbo, Members of the Free and Solidarni Members will support the bill submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u248	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report from the Committee on Education, Science and Youth on the Act amending the Law on the Rules of Finance for Science and some other laws, print No 1598. The first reading of the government draft law took place at a committee meeting on 22 June 2017. The Science Financing Act sets out the procedure for deciding on the distribution of funds between scientific units. The Minister of Science and Higher Education grants scientific units a scientific category, based on a comprehensive assessment of the quality of scientific or research and development activities of scientific units. The evaluation shall be carried out by the Scientific Units Evaluation Committee. At the same time, approximately 1 000 are assessed. scientific units. The uniform standard of assessment takes into account about 1 million scientific achievements presented by scientific bodies. The evaluation is carried out by about 200 experts from various fields. Surveys completed by scientific units cover more than 780 thousand. publications concern almost 33 600 scientific projects, almost 94 000. scientific staff. They also cover over 78,000. patented products and almost 67 thousand. the practical effects of scientific research. The Scientific Units Evaluation Committee shall define the reference units for scientific categories A and B. The comprehensive assessment used in the algorithm set out in the Regulation of the Minister for Science and Higher Education allows for objective allocation of funds. The competition procedure is not individualised. They're different from other administrative procedures. The Government's draft law amending the Law on the Principles for the Financing of Science and certain other laws aims at simplifying the way scientific entities apply for funding, removing the need to apply the procedures provided for in the Code of Administrative Procedure. The objective of this legal amendment did not raise any doubts other than that raised in the discussion as to whether the appeal procedure would be equally effective after the amendment was introduced. Four amendments were tabled to the draft law extending the possibility to exclude the application of k.p.a. and allowing the recovery of the debtor who has obtained the necessary funds prior to the limitation of the claim. The Commission has examined and accepted the amendments. The Committee on Education, Science and Youth presents to the High House the uniform text of the draft law amending the Law on the principles of funding for science and certain other laws, print No 1670, and recommends that the High House adopt it. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u252	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform has the honour to present its position on the government draft law amending the Law on the Principles of Science Financing and some other laws. In my short speech, I will focus on excluding certain provisions of the CCPA. from the application in the procedure for the award of the scientific category to scientific bodies, which is the subject of the regulation of Article. 47 Law on the principles of science financing. The new paragraph proposed by the designers. 5 in Article 5, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 47 excludes from application in cases relating to the award of the scientific category Article. 10 and Article 10 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers (OJ L 55, 28.2.2011, p. 1). 81 c.p.a. It should be recalled that in the article. 10 the principle of the party's active participation in the proceedings has been regulated and Article. 81 states that the facts can be regarded as proven if the party had the opportunity to comment on the evidence provided, unless there are special circumstances. As you can see, the projector seeks to limit the principle of active participation of the parties in this procedure, which is justified by the fact that the award of the scientific category to scientific entities - as is apparent from the provisions of the Act and the Regulation - takes place in a completely separate manner in which the rules of procedure must be uniform for all individuals. It follows from the specific nature of this procedure that it is not individualised, as is the case with typical administrative proceedings. This argument of the designer cannot be accepted. Contrary to its claim, the procedure for granting a category is individualised as it must be determined whether a specific scientific entity fulfils the conditions for granting a category for which it is applying or not. This unit - this is individualized. In the course of this procedure, errors may be made which cannot be remedied if the Article does not apply to that procedure. 81 c.p.a. Let me remind you, Art. 81 c.p.a. shall apply both to proceedings at first instance and to appeals. It is precisely by allowing the party to comment on the evidence it has made to avoid making mistakes and thus speed up the waiting time for the matter to be settled. Thus, we are dealing with a serious limitation of the rights of the party proposed by the projector, which will not only contribute to the improvement of the procedure but even make it more difficult. During the committee meeting, I mentioned this, and I remember my situation as a rector when one of the departments appealed. The case of the taking of evidence and the exercise of those rights which Article gives. 81, which is a development of the principle of the party's active participation, which is provided for in Article. Ten, it was extremely important there. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the above argument, I make an amendment to delete in Article. 1 in point 9 (1). 5. Here you go, and thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u268	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you very much for your comments. These topics were discussed at the meetings of the Sejm committees. In general, this amendment to the Law on the Principles of Science Financing and some other laws aims not only to accelerate or technical modifications in the issue and process of both KEJN and NCN and NCBiR, but also to adjust the regulation to reality. Why? Because, I'm going to start with KEJN, the process at KEJN is that the units are applying for parametrization now and that KEJN only processes on the basis of the documents that the entity itself will provide to it. It is difficult to talk about the parties to the proceedings here, because there is one party, that is, the applicant, who provides all the documents and therefore knows them. Hence the disposition of the article. 81, i.e. the possibility of getting acquainted with all the evidence, is fulfilled from the very beginning, that is, from the moment at which the entity submits this evidence. That is why this article appears to be a c.p.a. it simply does not apply in this case because there are no other parties to the proceeding that might not know all the evidence. In addition, all these documents are therefore known to the submitting body and all KEJN's opinions are public after the decision has been made and available to the individual in the electronic system, hence the principle of getting acquainted with the meaning of k.p.a. with the evidence is preserved, so this article does not need to be used here. Also, I would like to stress this very strongly, we do not change the way in which individuals will not be able to appeal. Of course, they will be able to appeal, do so, and do so on the basis of both KEJN's opinion and their data that they have submitted. Therefore, this appeal - and I would like to remind you here - both from a procedural and substantive point of view it is possible to proceed on the occasion of the evaluation of individuals and the deadline is quite long. Therefore, it seems that the introduction of additional... access to the evidence that the entity has submitted leads only to the obstruction of the procedure, the extension of the time of proceedings and may lead to the obstruction of the system. Also art. 10, i.e. the principle of the party's active participation in the proceedings, is ex definitione fulfilled because it is the entity that initiates the proceedings, so it is an active member of the proceedings. KEJN itself cannot initiate the procedure unless the entity so requests. Therefore, as regards the party's active participation in the proceedings, this requirement is here, seems to be fulfilled. It's a little pointless to copy this article again. As regards the redemption and the amount of the redemption of the funds, I would just like to remind you that we propose that under 150,000. it has been possible to redeem and spread over instalments by decision of the Director of the NCBiR. The amount of the grants granted by the government agency is several times higher, hence the amount of the redemption is appropriate to the resources granted by the government agencies and seems reasonable. Thank you, Mrs Joannie Schmidt. for that remark. Our intention is in no way to limit the funding of humanists. At the moment, in the National Humanities Development Programme, we have tens of millions dedicated to the development of humanities. We therefore, of course, accept this amendment. It seems acceptable and reasonable. As for legal doubts, the questions were raised by Mr Jarubas, of course we want to simplify the way decisions are made, and there are several thousand of these decisions in government agencies. Therefore, if we explicite enumeratively mention what should be in every decision, this is clearly a significant increase in human resources for each decision. And let me remind you, we're not shutting down an article that talks about giving information. I mean, every decision has to tell us why such a decision was taken and not another one. On the other hand, we would like to see that there are no enumeratively listed points required by C.P.A. in the adoption of an administrative decision. Of course, the issuing of these several thousand decisions, if they are issued according to such a list, will take much, much longer. This is also partly the answer to Mrs Cicholska's question. These changes will significantly accelerate the decision-making of both government agencies and KEJN. At the same time, this obligation of information in the decision as to why such a decision was taken will result in decisions being issued more quickly, they will be transparent to the addressees. Of course, we fully maintain the appeal procedure. In the appeal, the entity sees all the documentation of the procedure, all the decisions and opinions of the KEJN, therefore this process will be completely transparent and transparent for the applicant or for the applicant to the government agencies. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u274	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, I am pleased to present a report on the government draft law amending the Act on the Agricultural Market Agency and the organisation of certain agricultural markets and amending certain other laws on printing No 1649. The contested law first sets out the current state of play for school milk and school fruit and vegetables, which will be replaced by a single joint programme for schools. The Act creates a national legal basis, in accordance with European Union legislation, for a school programme which is necessary for the implementation of a new programme for the supply of fruit and vegetables and milk and milk products to educational establishments from the 2017/2018 school year. In addition, national provisions establishing a new institutional framework for support for the development of agriculture and rural areas for reducing the number of paying agencies operating in Poland, setting up a National Agricultural Support Centre, abolishing the Agricultural Market Agency and the Agricultural Property Agency, shall enter into force on 1 September 2017, while making use of the resources of these agencies by the National Agricultural Support Centre. In view of the above, the draft law in the context of the changes made to European Union legislation or national institutional changes related to the reduction of the number of paying agencies operating in Poland from two to one and the establishment of the National Agricultural Support Centre assumes the creation of a legal framework for the administration of the school programme. By the Act of 15 December 2016 on the prevention of unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage in trade in agricultural and food products, penalties were imposed on those who purchase agricultural products without a written contract or on the basis of a contract which does not fulfil certain conditions. The application of the above-mentioned provisions has shown the need to clarify them and remove interpretation doubts in order to ensure an even better functioning of the market. It is proposed to amend the Act on the Agricultural Market Agency and the organisation of certain agricultural markets, which clarifies the concept of producer. It should also be pointed out that Member States have the possibility to introduce provisions on the application of contracts for the proper functioning of the internal market. The farmer must be understood by the producer as a natural or legal person or group of natural or legal persons, irrespective of the legal status of such a group and its members under national law, whose agricultural holding is situated in the territory of the Republic of Poland and which carries out agricultural activities. Such clarification will require a contract for the supply of agricultural products when one of the parties is a farmer whose agricultural holding is located in Poland. At its meeting on 21 June 2017, the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development held a substantive discussion on the project. The Committee on Agriculture, after first reading and considering this draft law, requests the adoption of the attached draft. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u279	Thank you very much. Mr Jarosław Sachajko will now speak on behalf of the Kukiz club15.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u280	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I am pleased to present my position on the report of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the Government's draft law amending the Act on the Agricultural Market Agency and the organisation of certain agricultural markets and amending certain other laws. One of the main objectives of the proposed law is to clarify the provisions on the obligation to enter into written contracts for the supply of agricultural products, which, according to the Ministry, is to strengthen the position of the agricultural producer as the first link in the food supply chain. This objective must be considered to be highly appropriate. However, the question arises: Will the solutions proposed make them feasible? The obligation to conclude contracts for each supply of agricultural products to the first buyer has existed since 3 October 2015, i.e. since the entry into force of the amendment of the Act on the Agricultural Market Agency and the organisation of certain agricultural markets. This obligation is based on the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 17 December 2013 establishing a common organisation of the markets in agricultural products. The new regulations, so that they can function effectively and have the desired effect, should primarily seek to align the position of agricultural producers and processors and intermediaries. The aim is for both parties to be equal partners, who will be able to negotiate the terms of supply contracts - together with prices, deadlines, quantity and quality of products - on identical, equal terms. Meanwhile, contracts for the supply of goods by farmers are adhesion agreements, the terms of which are determined by a party with a stronger position, i.e. intermediaries, hypermarkets, foreign discounts. Such an agreement shall be implemented by accession, i.e. the farmer may accept the agreement previously drawn up, the content of which is not subject to negotiation, or cancel its conclusion and look elsewhere for an offer to dispose of its products. The problem will grow because intermediary companies are growing in strength. More and more of them become monopolists on the market. However, the position of farmers remains unchanged and the mandatory conclusion of contracts will not change their situation. The farmer is never guaranteed that the fruits of his several months' work will be sold at a price that will cover, for example, the cost of production. It is clear that farmers are aware of the risks of their profession and of many different factors, such as weather conditions, which affect the profitability of their business. However, this does not justify the current problem of farmers, which is their low negotiating capacity, their weak position in the food supply chain. The Ministry, when proposing mandatory contracts, penalties for buyers or supervision by the Agricultural Market Agency, does not strengthen the position of farmers, but merely consolidates the current state of affairs, recognising farmers as a weaker party to the agreement. I would like to make it clear that I do not think that the proposed regulations are bad, but I regard them as too shallow, unrecoverable issues and unchangeable contractual relations between the agricultural producer and the processor or supplier. To conclude, I would like to suggest to the Ministry an exceptionally good solution that will allow the development of small and medium-sized farms. This will allow for one simple step, namely the authorisation to sell agricultural products in the framework of agricultural retail trade to local shops, canteens, restaurants. The bill allowing such sales was submitted by the Kukiz club15 and I hope that the High House will soon lean over it. And today I am making an amendment to your Marshal, which will make life easier for farmers and small shops who die in Poland from 5 to 7 thousand a year. Thank you.

The transfer of these and other activities from ARiMR to KOWR results in increased costs and the need to implement accreditation procedures in the new entity. One paying agency is therefore fiction. This shows that this was not the purpose of the government which decided to liquidate the Agricultural Market Agency and the Agricultural Property Agency. It's just a question of why we're actually doing this on installments. We have been working on a single bill in this area quite recently, and today we are working on another one, going back to the same subject. Is it the inability of the government and its representatives to correct their own mistakes in the short term, or is it a deliberate act to conceal the real purpose for which one agency is being liquidated to delegate tasks to other centres? Is it really the purpose of staff exchanges? These mechanisms, which are to be delegated, are among others. monitoring the buying-in of milk in Poland, implementing a programme of support for beekeeping, monitoring production and charges on the sugar market, administering production potential on the wine and wine market, carrying out quota operations for milk and milk products in 2014 and 2015 and transmitting data to the Central Statistical Office. Of course, our doubts as to the previous regulations relating to this present law have raised earlier and further the need to sign contracts for the sale of agricultural products between farmers and buyers. Actually, it's thousands of deals. The question is whether it is necessary for farmers to do so, because it is actually a little different from that of the Law and Justice. They're treating it as their next unnecessary responsibilities. So far, they have worked perfectly well with their recipients without such unnecessary obligations. So what is the real purpose of this new obligation for the farmer and the company that receives the goods from him? Will it really end with these agreements protecting farmers themselves, or will they be a nuisance to them? I will remind you that a farmer can very often no longer afford to rent a law firm today, which will check whether the contract is safe for him, and the farmer himself sometimes does not have to understand the complex legal terms which may be contained in such a contract. Farmers often ask me at meetings why other areas of the economy are exempt from such obligations, and the sale of agricultural products is thus to be regulated and, in their opinion, simply controlled. So that sometimes, in order to do the right thing, you can fire a baby with a bath. Under the cloak of introducing facilities and better conditions for the sale of agricultural products to farmers, they can actually be put into a lot of trouble. Such a solution will certainly improve its start. I have already expressed my support for the creation of KOWR, which compensates for the powers of the two other institutions that have been liquidated. Therefore, I do not raise any objections to the transfer to the NEBRO of certain competences previously belonging to the AriMR. I am also very pleased with the clarification by the designers of regulations relating to the contracting of agricultural products. The amendment provides for the abolition of this obligation in the case of small counterparties, e.g. those dealing with direct sales. As the applicants point out, it is necessary to specify the entries in this area because of the current differences in interpretation and therefore the new provisions do not raise my objections. They will certainly reduce red tape and are in line with the industry's expectations. I also consider it reasonable and good to introduce an obligation to keep contracts for control purposes for a period of 2 years. The committee meeting also raised the issue of the introduction of quota thresholds, after which the obligation to conclude and store contracts arises and the penalties imposed only on purchasers in the event of failure to conclude contracts or incorrect contracts. In my view, the exemption in this project from the obligation to contract small transactions, as is the case with direct sales, is an expected facilitation for small suppliers. However, I am not entirely convinced by the Ministry's argument regarding the imposition of sanctions on only one of the parties to the transaction. The use of a similar mechanism in other industries must not be the only reason for its duplication. In my opinion, the consequences of failing to comply with the provisions of the Act should be borne by both sellers and buyers. I hope that this issue will be raised during further work on the amendment. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding this project.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u324	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy on the governmental draft law amending the Real Estate Management Act and some other laws, print Nos 1560 and 1678. The aim of the project is to organise certain areas of real estate management, and in particular professional activities in this area. One of the most important changes proposed in the project is the definition of real estate management and real estate intermediation activities. This will ensure a transparent and stable situation for real estate brokers and real estate managers. As a result of the too far-reaching deregulation of the real estate broker and the real estate manager, which entered into force on 1 January 2014, interpretation doubts were raised in practice from both the intermediaries and the managers as well as the clients of those entities. The real estate broker and the real estate manager have also been defined as entrepreneurs to reduce the risk that people will be dealt with in a completely random way through or through management. The proposed rules are very expected by professional environments related to the management of real estate. The rules on the management of immovable property by public authorities are provided for, inter alia, the exemption of perpetual users with a subsequent land transfer agreement for the perpetual use of the same property from the obligation to pay the first fee, as they paid the first fee when concluding the contract which expired. At the same time, it was possible to conclude without tendering a contract for perpetual use with persons who were perpetual users of these properties, but had not been able to submit a request for the extension of perpetual use before the expiry date of that right. The proposed rules will also ensure uniform practice in the sale of public property to persons who lease or use public property for many years. It also introduced more favourable rules for determining the amount of the Adiacen fee for the increase in the value of the property caused by the construction of technical infrastructure facilities for persons who voluntarily contributed funds to the construction of these facilities. The project was submitted to the Sejm on 17 May 2017 on 24 May 2017 for first reading in the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy. At its joint meeting on 7 June 2017, the committees decided to refer the draft law to work in an emergency subcommittee. The subcommittee meeting took place on 20 June 2017 and on 4 July 2017 the joint committees adopted the subcommittee report. During the meeting, all the proposals envisaged in the draft were discussed. Several legislative and editorial amendments tabled by the Legislative Office of the Sejm were adopted by the joint committees during the project process. The parliamentary amendment to the article was also accepted. 2 projects, i.e. to the Law on geodetic and cartographical law, aimed at extending the possibility for the competent authorities to carry out a basic map before 1 January 2014 until 31 December 2023. Another accepted amendment on the addition of an article in the draft. 9, is the consequence of the adoption of a change in geodetic and cartographic law. The amendment to the article was also accepted. Article 6 (6) (a) 2 of the draft amendment. It was clarified that the application of the parties to the brokerage contract and the property management contract concluded before the date of entry into force of the new provisions for the transfer of a copy of the insurance document should be submitted in writing. Amendments were tabled during the committee meeting. Amendment 1 concerns the withdrawal of amendments to Article. 37 Act on Real Estate Management. The revised provision provides for, among others, the possibility of concluding contracts for the acquisition of real estate with persons who were perpetual users of these properties on a non-trade-free basis, but did not request the extension of perpetual use before the expiry date of the law and declare their willingness to continue to use the real estate. Amendment 2 concerns the withdrawal from the amendment of the provision of Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with the opinion of the European Parliament and of the Council concerning the placing of plant protection products on the market and the placing of plant protection products on the market and the placing of plant protection products on the market. 2 The Real Estate Management Act, which proposes that the analysis and compilations of the real estate market drawn up by the Minister responsible for construction, planning and spatial planning and housing should be made available to everyone without any charge. Amendment 3 concerns the provision of real estate managers with access to registers and documents containing property data. Amendment 4 concerns the repeal of the transitional provision, Article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1, introducing a general rule on the application of new rules to pending and pending cases. Amendment 5 refers to the repeal of Article. 8 projects to maintain the existing composition of the Committee on Professional Responsibility and the State Qualifications Committee until new compositions of these committees have been established, but no longer than 6 months. These amendments were not supported by the joint committees. They were reported as minority proposals. On behalf of the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy, I request that the draft law submitted by the committees be adopted. Thank you very much.
