ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u325	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Tomasz Ławniczak, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u326	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! The proposal for a law on amending the Real Estate Management Act considered by the High House has several objectives to achieve. Firstly, clarification of concepts, because their divergent interpretation by government and local authorities, and even by courts, caused confusion in the decision-making process, and among citizens could give rise to a sense of lack of transparency of law. Precise definitions of real estate management, real estate intermediation and public property management are introduced. Provisions were also clarified to allow for the first update of the annual fee of the costs incurred by the perpetual user for the construction of individual technical infrastructure facilities, because there were different interpretations in this area, which could open the way to irregularities. Secondly, the bill rationalises the conduct of public administration e.g. by adding a provision relating to the possibility of remission of monetary claims due to the Treasury where the costs of enforcement exceed the amount of the claim itself, including interest. The same applies to deferment and distribution of payments on instalments. Thirdly, the draft law harmonises the conduct of public administration, both government and self-government, which will have a positive impact on the persons and tenants of the State Treasury's real estate. This applies e.g. the introduction of a uniform practice of public property disposal for their long-term tenants or the harmonisation of a favourable mechanism for determining the Adiacen fee for persons who voluntarily contributed funds to the construction of technical infrastructure facilities. Fourthly, the project also provides insurance for persons and public property leasers against an irregularity in the valuation carried out by the valuer. In general, the draft amendment to the Real Estate Management Act uses more precise language with regard to existing solutions, harmonising procedures for public administration, making them clearer for citizens, and protecting tenants themselves from the effects of possible miscalculations in the valuation of immovable property. Hence Law and Justice will support the bill under consideration. Mr. Marshal! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I also propose the following amendment to the bill. We propose that in the article. 1 in point 31 in point (a) b semicolon replace with a comma and add lithium. c as follows: The Minister responsible for construction, planning and spatial planning and housing shall establish and publish applicable professional standards by means of a notice. What is the justification for this conclusion? Namely, every valuer is obliged to apply the law and, among others, to carry out professional activities. professional standards. Standards shall be established by professional organisations of property experts in agreement with the Minister of Infrastructure and Construction. This means that the standard-setting initiative is on the side of professional organisations, while the Minister ensures that they comply with the law. But during almost 13 years of application of this provision, which grants professional organisations the privilege of setting professional standards, only one standard has been agreed and there is no indication that another standard will be agreed in the near future. The lack of standards has a negative impact on the quality of real estate valuations and at the same time makes it difficult for public administrations and courts to assess the evidence of estimated operations in the proceedings. In addition, inadequately drawn opinions on the value of immovable property result in unnecessary extension of administrative and judicial proceedings due, of course, to various types of appeals. It is therefore proposed that the establishment of professional standards be taken over by the Minister of Infrastructure and Construction as the body supervising the professional activities of property experts. This will speed up the work on standards and correct the shortcomings of today's standard setting mechanism, which should be considered inefficient. It is proposed that the Minister announces the content of established professional standards by means of a notice in the Official Journal. Thank you kindly. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u362	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position on the draft law amending the Law on State aid in the acquisition of the first apartment by young people. The most important amendment proposed in the draft law is the change in value - expressed as a percentage in the available envelope - of the current 95% 100%. The analysis of the statistics on the use of funds in subsequent years of the programme shows that the 95 per cent threshold laid down by the Act has been exceeded over the previous four years. The additional amendment is formal in nature and will allow for the renewal of the selection of applications. The provisions in force provide that where the total amount resulting from the application for own contribution with the payment of the support from the Fund for a given year reaches the equivalent of 95% the amount planned for the payment of financial support, the Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego announces in the Public Information Bulletin that applications for own contribution have been suspended and that the crediting institutions refrain from receiving such requests from the day following the date of publication of the information by BGK. In the legislator's assumptions, setting a threshold of 95% as a safety limit the value of the funding envelope for a given year was intended to allow the other funds to be transferred for the payment of additional financing in the form of repayment of part of the loan to borrowers in the cases indicated related to the enlargement of the family. Several years' practice in the operation of the programme has shown that blocking the acceptance of applications at 95% resulted in the use of funding from this programme to the following extent: in 2004 - 34% of the 600 million statutory limit, in 2015 - 84% 715 million statutory limit, in 2016 - 96% statutory limit of PLN 730 million and in 2017 - 91% the statutory limit of 746 million. This is a situation distorting the idea of the programme as well as saving a specific amount in the State budget for a given purpose. However, the 2016 example shows that more than 8 million have been spent over 95% the 2016 envelope It can be concluded from this that the amount of additional benefits was significantly higher, since, as the example of previous years shows, not all applications end with the signing of a credit agreement. The Act also provides for a mechanism in the event of the exhaustion of funds for these payments. It assumes in the article. Article 36 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 6 that applications for repayment of part of the credit under Article. 8 will, in the event of exhaustion of appropriations in a given year, be implemented as soon as the BGK receives the first tranche in the following year. However, due to the end of the programme on 30 September 2018, the next year will not be available. It should therefore be considered to leave a limit which would guarantee the payment of appropriations under Article. 8. Maybe a 5% limit. is too big, as the current statistics show, while setting a threshold of 100% as proposed in the draft amending act, it is also subject to risk. In view of the above, such a limit could be expressed e.g. 98%, which - taking into account the expected increase in requests for repayment of part of the credit under Article. 8 - would save some resources for this purpose. Despite some of the above doubts, however, I would like to support this bill on behalf of the Modern Club and inform the High House that we will vote as a Modern for yes. At the same time, I would like to call on the PiS to consider today, not waiting until the end of this project, the programme, the possibility of re-devising the same programme, because - as all the honourable speakers have said - the matter related to housing, especially for young marriages, is absolutely priority in our country. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u390	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Dear Minister, I have the honour to present a report from the Public Finance Commission on print No 1701 on the Government bill amending the Personal Income Tax Act and the Corporate Income Tax Act, print No 1602. At its 44th meeting, held on 21 June 2017, the Sejm, in accordance with Article 4 of the Rules of Procedure. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Committee on Public Finances for consideration. The main objective of the Act is to create a mechanism to stimulate investments made by entrepreneurs. The proposed instrument will provide the possibility of annual single settlement of fixed capital expenditure up to 100,000. PLN with a minimum value of 10 thousand. In addition, it extends the range of entities that will be able to benefit from this settlement as an instrument not covered by the de minimis aid regime. It can also be used by entrepreneurs who have benefited in recent years e.g. from European Union funding. The possibility of using a one-off depreciation mechanism will encourage the use of spending on new machinery and equipment that will improve the competitiveness of businesses and ensure better paid jobs. The instrument will provide incentives both for operating companies and for those planning to set up a business. The Public Finance Commission, after considering the above draft law at its meeting on 5 July 2017, requests the Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u392	Good morning. However, this settlement will not concern investments in means of transport and real estate, and the aim of such a limitation is inter alia. the need to raise interest in investment in modernising and modernising jobs and implementing improved, innovative solutions in this area. These solutions are rather designed for small and medium-sized entrepreneurs and start-ups. One-off annual accounts of investment expenditure will be limited by a limit and must not exceed 100 000. PLN and must be at least 10,000 in a given year. zł. Today's project also provides for changes in the collection of income tax from revenues obtained in Poland by foreign air navigation companies, and the essence of these changes is the exemption of domestic economic operators from the obligation to pay 10% tax on revenues from air tickets. These changes are due both to the difficulties associated with the implementation of this commitment and in some cases to the total impossibility of implementation. Of course, we will support this law, because even with these selectively designated fixed assets it allows for some convenience and a one-off deduction into the costs of the company, but this reluctance from the rulers to make minor changes is simply incomprehensible. The amendment prepared by the Ministry of Finance regarding the provisions of the Tax Order provides, first, for the arrangement of relations between the Director of National Tax Information and the Head of National Tax Administration by eliminating the need for the Director of National Tax Information to automatically direct questions in cases where he already has the opinion of the Head of National Tax Administration on the same facts related to tax avoidance. The new rules will help to improve the operation of the IT system, in which the Register of Revenues will be kept. The bill also provides for a revision of the provisions of the Public Finance Act to exclude the participation of the Public Prosecutor's Office in issuing opinions on draft contracts and unilateral legal actions carried out by the State Treasury. The proposed regulation will contribute to improving the implementation of the State budget, financing the borrowing needs of the State budget, the management of the Treasury debt and the liquidity of the State budget, the need for their ongoing, immediate implementation. The draft law also provides for the amendment of the provisions of the Act introducing the Act on National Tax Administration in respect of the transfer of the separate portion of the budget of the National Tax Administration from 1 January 2019. This amendment will improve the functioning of the National Tax Administration in the financial and accounting area and allow for the design of a comprehensive budget of the National Tax Administration for 2019 in a separate part of the State budget. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u420	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The amendments to the Tax Order concern two issues. I'd say they're tidy. Firstly, they concern the opinion of the application for individual interpretation and the regulation of the relationship between the Director of National Tax Information and the Head of National Tax Administration. The aim is not to repeat the interpretation activities if the facts or the event correspond to an issue which has already been the subject of an earlier opinion. Secondly, the project facilitates the implementation of the provisions of the Tax Order as regards the keeping of a register of tax pledges. In the light of the proposed change, it will be possible to keep the register by entrusting this activity to another body of the National Tax Administration, I understand that at a lower level, by the head of the National Tax Administration. We consider the proposed changes to be a good and simple proposal to improve the functioning of the National Tax Administration. But unfortunately, I can no longer give a similar opinion on the revision of the Public Finance Act. The Government proposes to exclude the application of Article. Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Act of 15 December 2016 on the Public Prosecutor's Office, which imposes an obligation to obtain a legal opinion of the Public Prosecutor's Office on draft contracts and legal acts carried out by the Treasury if the value of the subject matter of legal action exceeds PLN 100 million. Liberalisation in this respect does not seem justified. Issues related to the implementation of the State budget in terms of public spending, financing of loan needs, borrowing commitments, management of public funds and management of public debt are too serious to deal with the provisions relating to the opinion of the Public Prosecutor General. We are observing the government's actions on sovereign debt, for example, when establishing a budget deficit at a very risky and dangerous border, the loosening of the principle of expenditure - under these conditions it is unlikely that there can be any further loosening of the rules and resignation from the opinion of the Attorney General on such important issues, after all, relating to the security of public finances, with the security of our country. The reduction in the cost of debt service is here, I find, too weak, and even a funny motivation to abolish the principle of financial opinion by the Attorney General. The next proposed amendment to the provisions implementing the National Tax Administration Act concerns the extension of the time limit for the separation of the budget for the National Tax Administration for the KAS only from 1 January 2019. I must say that this need to extend this term, too, seems to have no justification. We will express our position in the vote, although if there were a declaration of clear change regarding the failure to resign from the opinion of the Attorney General, we would be able to be in favour of transferring the project to work. At the next committee meeting on 22 June 2017, we introduced a number of legislative amendments to the original draft of this law. The draft law provides for an amendment of the article. 400j Act of 27 April 2001 Environmental Law. The changes concern the rules for the appointment and cancellation of the management boards of the regional environmental funds. The proposed amendments are the development of the rules by adding a provision whereby, in the event of a failure to comply with the obligation to appoint or dismiss members of the management board of the voivodship funds, the voivodship shall take over its competence, which shall apply for the appointment or cancellation of members of the voivodship boards of the funds. A similar solution is also introduced if the management board of the voivodship is to delay the appointment of members of the management board of the voivodship environmental and water management fund. During the committee work, an appropriate period of time was also devoted to the examination of the article. 3 of the Act in question, which states that the Act shall enter into force on the date of its publication. Such a solution, in the view of the applicants, is dictated by the fact that many supervisory boards and voivodship boards which have not previously been approved in this area specifically, intentionally delay the submission and processing of applications for the cancellation of old and the appointment of new voivodship boards of funds, which they are obliged to do by previous regulations. Regional funds have a major impact on the level and effectiveness of the implementation of many programmes, including the use of European funds. Another destruction, according to the applicants, should not be tolerated and it is therefore advisable to adopt urgently the proposed bill. Mr. Marshal, High Izbo, the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry requests the High House to adopt the draft Act amending the Environmental Law in its report, in print No. 1668. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u448	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Joint Committees: the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy and the Committee on European Union Affairs, in relation to the Government's draft law amending the Law on the rules for implementing cohesion policy programmes financed in the financial perspective 2014 It's a 1636 sejm print. On 22 June this year. The High Sejm referred this draft law to the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy and the Commission on European Union Affairs. The joint committee on which the draft law was examined was held on 4 July. The committees, after considering the bill, ask for its adoption by the High Sejm. The draft law is an act of utmost importance for the efficient and efficient use of EU funds. The solutions contained in the proposal for a law submitted to the High House are the result of a broad discussion with the social partners, local governments, institutions for the implementation of EU funds, as well as beneficiaries of EU funds. The proposed amendment is the result of 2 years of experience in implementing the operational programmes of cohesion policy in the financial perspective of 2014. The proposed statutory amendments aim at simplifying the implementation of projects financed by the beneficiaries for bodies applying for Union funds, facilitating the implementation of projects funded by them, and improving the implementation of operational programmes by the various institutions involved in their implementation. The proposed changes, which aim to facilitate access to EU funds for potential beneficiaries, are to simplify the project evaluation process by removing the formal evaluation phase in favour of one de facto substantive evaluation phase. In contrast, formal conditions have been introduced by the institution. The applicant will be able to complete their shortcomings and make adjustments in the course of the competition. Another proposed improvement is the possibility to divide the competition into rounds. This means that the applicant will be able to properly and correctly prepare the application for funding and submit it in one of the rounds of the competition. A very important amendment is also a proposal to limit the scope of the Code of Administrative Procedure and to make the forms of communication between the applicant and the competent institution more flexible by giving the possibility to choose the form of contact: electronic or written. The amendment also introduces the protection of intellectual property of projects prepared by applicants by prohibiting them from being made available during the competition procedure. It will also make it easier for EU applicants to prohibit the competent institutions from requiring certificates and documents from those bodies to which the institution will be able to see through the tools and instruments available to it - public registers. In order to speed up the evaluation of projects in the appeal procedure, the deadlines foreseen for the examination of the protest were significantly reduced and the possibility for the applicant to withdraw the protest was introduced. The amendment introduces a new institution: an ombudsman of European funds who will take care of the affairs of all stakeholders in European Union funds. It will be among others. responsible for the collection of proposals and requests related to the implementation of operational programmes which will improve the implementation system. An important change, which is foreseen in the draft law, is the possibility of amending the grant agreement. The amendment of the contract will be possible if it does not affect the fulfilment of the project selection criteria in such a way as to result in a negative assessment of the project. The draft law also limits the number of documents, programming guidelines, which beneficiaries will apply under the signed grant agreement. In order to facilitate and ensure the smooth implementation of partner projects, the possibility of changing partners in the partner project was envisaged, with the agreement of the competent institution, of course. In addition, voivodships will be involved in the implementation of the operational programmes in order to improve the implementation of the operational programmes. In the course of the work of the joint committees, amendments of the editorial, legislative and regulatory nature have been tabled and adopted, as well as substantive amendments. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 1 point 5 of the amendment consists of the waiver of the obligation to publish notices on the detailed descriptions of the priority axes of the operational programme in the Polish Monitor and the provincial official journal. Art. 1 point 30 and point 36 - amendment consisting of a clear indication of the 14-day time limit which the applicant will be entitled to lodge a complaint in the event that the protest is left unanswered as a result of the failure to remedy the deficiencies in the protest. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Paragraph 39 is replaced by the expression "obligation' in the provision on the extent to which an expert should inform the competent institution of the absence of circumstances excluding it from the possibility of carrying out tasks. Art. 4 introduces amendments to the Local Government Act to organise and redefine the proposed regulation on the possibility of creating regional development funds. Art. 7 point 2 amends the Act on the creation of the Polish Agency for Enterprise Development by clarifying the rules for the appointment of members of sectoral councils. Art. Point 12 amends the Public Finance Act by clearly indicating in the material provision the principle that transfers to the register of entities excluded from receiving European funds will take place through the ePUAP public administration platform, excluding information provided to beneficiaries. Article 1 is also clarified. 23, transitional provision, by unambiguous indication... Such provision has been made that enforcement proceedings concerning Union measures initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of the Act will take place on the basis of existing rules. A new transitional provision has also been added, which provides for the obligation to publish communications on the detailed descriptions of the priority axes of the operational programme in the Polish Monitor and the provincial official journal, according to which communications signed by the competent authorities before the date of entry into force of the Act will be published on the existing basis. And finally, art. 30 sets the date of entry into force of the Act and sets out the provisions which will enter into force on 1 September 2017 following the change of state, i.e. the entry into force of the Education Law Act and the Act on the National Agricultural Support Centre. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! All amendments presented by me to the Act, which will be the subject of the vote of the High House, are both changes from the Government's submission and proposals for amendments adopted by the committees, taking into account the proposals made by the Legislative Office of the Sejm. In the course of the committee's work, the draft law was further tabled by the representative of the Modern and the representative of the Civic Platform by the amendment concerning the proposed solutions for the elimination of the programming guidelines, the powers of the Ombudsman of European funds, financial instruments and feedback, the regulation on the improvement of the application for funding in the course of the evaluation by the committee, the evaluation of projects, the cancellation of time limits in the review procedure, the participation of voivodeships in the implementation of regional operational programmes, the regulation enabling changes in the grant agreement, changes in the institution of the territorial contract. Following a very lively discussion and a comprehensive explanation by the Government representative, these amendments were not accepted and were rejected as a result of the vote, with the Modern Representative requesting that the amendments he tabled be considered to be minority requests, which I am requesting to be rejected. In presenting the above, bearing in mind that the Committee on Public Finance has given a positive opinion on this project and that the joint committees, the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy and the European Union's Committee on Affairs, have adopted the draft as presented today before the High Chamber, I would like to ask for its adoption. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u449	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Please take the floor of Mr Lech Kołakowski. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u450	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the Government's draft law amending the Law on the rules for implementing cohesion policy programmes financed in the financial perspective 2014 and 2020 and certain other laws. It pursues the essential objective of improving the use of European funds. The proposed changes are the result of 2 years of experience in the implementation of operational programmes 2014 and 2020 and take into account both the perspective of applicants for funding from the Funds applying, beneficiaries who have already implemented and cleared projects funded by the European Union, as well as the institutions responsible for implementing operational programmes. The most important improvements introduced by the provisions of this Act include the introduction of a simplified procedure for verifying formal conditions, the possibility of making extensive use of statements from applicants for funding, which will simplify and speed up the evaluation of projects. Then, allowing for the possibility to correct projects during their evaluation respecting the principle of equal treatment of applicants, so as to finance the best possible projects, do not reject good projects due to errors that may still be corrected during the evaluation. Another is the acceleration of the project, the signing of contracts for the implementation of projects through the possibility of organising competitions in rounds, shortening the time limits for the review procedure, so that the applicant has a real chance of obtaining funding for his project in case of errors in the initial assessment carried out by the institution. It will then ensure a high quality evaluation of projects by involving only workers with appropriate competence, facilitating the clearance of the received grant by extending the deadline by which the advance received can be settled without sanctioning the payment of interest for late settlement. This further reduces the practice of binding beneficiaries in co-financing agreements with records of documents which are not addressed by eliminating the programming guidelines. Then simplifying the implementation of the partnership projects by abolishing the ban on the creation of partnerships by related parties and introducing the possibility of changing the partnership when necessary. In addition, this law introduces a new institution, which is extremely important for all those interested in the efficient functioning of the system of implementation of EU funds, which is the spokesman for European funds. The Ombudsman's primary role through notifications from stakeholders will be to identify barriers and obstacles and to make proposals for improvements that can facilitate the implementation of operational projects and programmes. All the changes proposed in the draft law aim to remove barriers to access to EU funds and shift the emphasis of the functioning implementation system, so that the substantive issues, rather than the formal ones, will give rise to support and that the work of applicants resulting from the existing procedures is as small as possible to concentrate their efforts primarily on the substantive preparation of valuable projects which will contribute to the social and economic development of Poland. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law amending the Law on the rules for implementing cohesion policy programmes financed in the financial perspective 2014 and 2020 and certain other laws. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u452	Mr. Marshal! Minister! The proposed amendment, as stated by the explanatory memorandum, is intended to respond to requests and requests made by institutions and bodies involved in the implementation and spending of funds from the financial perspective of 2014.2020 and aims to improve and ensure the efficient use of EU funds. We emphasized the positives of the project at first reading. However, it should be noted that a number of proposals from local governments - institutions implementing regional operational programmes have not been taken into account and the project has not received a positive opinion from the Commission of the Joint Government and the Territorial Government. In addition, local government representation was not allowed to vote during the process of the project by the Committee on Local Government and Regional Policy and the Commission on European Union Affairs. This is surprising, given that the amendment will result in a series of implementing rules for both national and regional programmes funded by European funds. We know that the efficient and efficient spending of EU funds requires a harmonious interaction between the beneficiaries and the managing authority, so both parties' expectations of changes with a significant impact on the process of financial support applications should be treated equally. The fact that there has been no consensus between the government party and the local government party raises our concerns about the success of the amendment and we therefore make a number of amendments to the draft aimed at removing the raised objections. They address a number of key issues. First of all, the programming guidelines. They constitute an important instrument for the managing authority to concentrate the support expected. After all, the beneficiaries received signals that the programming guidelines were a kind of guide to the RPO for them. Their liquidation is not justified, it will even impede the implementation of projects, it will require the reconstruction of contracts, rules for cooperation with beneficiaries, especially in the area of project settlement. This will result in an increase in labour expenditure and chaos due to the need to annex hundreds of contracts concluded with beneficiaries. The consequence of the changes will be a weakening of the role of the managing authority in the implementation of the funds for which it is responsible. Financial instruments and repayable assistance. We have already argued that the funds earmarked for the implementation of financial instruments returned by final recipients do not constitute the income of the authorising officer or any entity to whom the authorising officer could entrust or commission the implementation of financial instruments or feedback support. The proposed provisions do not eliminate the problem of who in fact owns the financial resources returning from the financial instruments after their involvement. We are opposed to a solution where the funds coming back from the financial instruments no longer constitute the resources of the operational programmes and are therefore no longer EU funds. We point out that the way they are developed cannot be the subject of regulation of this law. Inclusion of the voivodship in the system of implementation of regional operational programmes. Delegating the voivodship to carry out checks to confirm compliance with the designation criteria and to carry out ad hoc checks is contrary to the provisions of the Partnership Agreement, which has defined the institutional system of cohesion policy. The role of the designation was reserved only for the Minister responsible for regional development and the audit authority. It should also be noted that since the dissolution of the departments of the voivodship offices, which in the perspective of 2007 and 2014 acted as intermediaries in the certification of payments, the voivodships do not have staff capable of carrying out the planned tasks. In addition, giving the voivodeship the right to be personally or by a representative to participate in the committee's evaluation of projects is in fact aimed at generating potential situations of conflict between the voivodship and the managing authority. Perhaps the projector depends on such a situation, because the voivodship as a supervisory authority exercises its functions in this respect at the stage of examining the legality of resolutions of the executive body of the voivodship local government, through which the decisions of the competition are adopted. In conclusion, on the issues raised, the project undermines the independence of regional authorities in the management of regional operational programmes established with the European Commission. Failure to take into account the reservations of local governments of voivodships as to the ambiguity of the proposed solutions and the possibility of complications in the relationship of the beneficiary - the managing authority is concerned that the declared objective of the law will not be achieved to a large extent. I hereby submit amendments to the draft. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u462	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the draft law amending the Law on the rules for implementing cohesion policy programmes financed in the financial perspective 2014 and 2020 and certain other laws, print No 1636. Representatives of the Ministry of Development and local government agree that the implementation of cohesion policy in the 2014-2020 perspective is a much more difficult task than in the previous programming period, i.e. in the years 2007 and 2013. The proposed solutions to simplify processes related to the use of EU funds are the result of requests made by institutions and bodies involved in spending European funds. According to estimates by the Ministry of Development, some PLN 100 billion may go to local governments in the 2014-2020 perspective, of which some 65 billion in regional operational programmes. The bill is an element of the so-called law. the business constitution, which together with the package of 100 changes for companies, aims to foster the development of entrepreneurship and innovation. We are convinced that the proposed law will contribute to reducing the bureaucratic burden on potential beneficiaries, among others. by reducing the number of documents necessary to obtain European funding, which applicants will be required to submit together with a request for funding, and will improve the implementation of projects, which should translate into a greater interest in applying for EU money and the higher quality of the projects notified. In the framework of the solutions submitted by the Ministry of Development, changes in competition procedures deserve special attention. Individual competitions will be able to be divided into rounds, which means that companies that do not have time to submit a competition application at a given date will be able to do so in the next round. It will also be possible to improve the proposal. The selection authority shall contact the applicant to make the relevant changes and the application shall not be rejected at the initial stage. It will facilitate the exemption of applicants from the obligation to provide documents which the institution could obtain itself, for example. certificates from the tax office or the Social Security Office or data which can be obtained from publicly available registers. The limitation of the possibility of requesting documents from the beneficiary to which the institution has access of its own motion is de facto a change in the philosophy of action, where the burden resulting from certain provisions, as far as possible, will rest on the public entity and not on the private entity. In addition, the programming guidelines were not included. The beneficiary who will sign the agreement will be bound by only the guidelines issued by the Minister for Development and the Minister for Finance to the extent strictly indicated. The obligation to analyze a large number of documents will disappear. Each application for project funding will be assessed first in terms of compliance with the formal conditions. If the institution finds that there are deficiencies in the application, it shall call on the applicant to complete them and the application may be addressed to the substantive assessment. Documents submitted to the institutions during the competition proceedings will not be made available to the public, this will ensure that all information contained in applications submitted by applicants is protected. Within the framework of simplifications, the Ministry of Development proposes the establishment of an institution for the Ombudsman of European funds. He will be responsible, among others. for the collection of proposals and requests related to the implementation of operational programmes which will improve the implementation system. The institution of the Ombudsman of the European Funds is an interesting concept, which may prove to be a breakthrough in the relationship between projectors and beneficiaries with institutions implementing operational programmes. It is important that the draft law recognises the need to build a more customer-friendly attitude towards institutions and proposes a space in which private entities can make their reservations about the functioning of the institution and its rules in a structured manner, and the institution itself undertakes to consider them in good faith. The draft law contains a number of provisions that can make it easier for entrepreneurs to apply for funding for projects and later implementation. The proposals of the Ministry of Finance are supported by an analysis of the needs and barriers faced by companies seeking EU funds. What is very important, in the proposed amendment, the interest of the beneficiaries was placed first, in the interest of the institutions involved in the implementation of the operational programmes. [[♪ The bell ♪]] We fully support the proposals contained in the proposed amendment to the Act, considering them to be in line with the real needs of the beneficiaries. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The Free and Solidarni Circle will support this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u470	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! First of all, thank you very much for the debate, for your comments and for your questions. Next, I would like to emphasise very clearly why we are introducing these changes in the implementation law. First of all, in order to simplify the functioning of the funds and to make these funds much more beneficiary-friendly, i.e. those entities that implement most of the EU projects. I am thinking here mainly of entrepreneurs, hence these proposed changes are part of the business constitution, and it is also about local governments, NGOs. That was the primary target. Thanks to this, we hope that the funds will be spent more efficiently, but also - even more importantly - that we will have better results. Because one of the basic paradigms, which is the principle that we want to introduce very strongly at the moment, is that funds are invested and not just spent. And that's the general rule. It comes from the experience of previous perspectives. It was also the result of the audit that we carried out in November and December 2015 on this new financial perspective. And this audit has clearly shown that such changes will be necessary for implementation. Part of the changes that did not have any legal consequences, i.e. they did not require changes in laws, we consistently introduced them in 2016, while those changes which required legal changes in this law are the subject of this debate today. From here, for example. if we had a dilemma by changing whether to take a solution on the part of the implementing body, the managing body or the beneficiary, we have always been on the side of the beneficiary. An example of this can be such changes as e.g. shortening the appeal procedure from 60 to 45 days - all the institutions were against it, also our government, and yet we introduce it, so that it was not only about local governments - or for example. the elimination of the programming guidelines. These guidelines have so far been issued by managing authorities not only at regional but also at national level. All the institutions were against it, too, and we are removing these programming guidelines. Another example of the Ombudsman, which is to represent the interests of the beneficiaries more than those of the managing authorities. It must also be said honestly that this is a solution that was very appealing to the beneficiaries, while most managing authorities did not like it, either from a regional or a national level. I'll remind you again. Between 2004 and 2006, in the first financial perspective, it was the voivodship offices that managed funds at regional level in general. That's where we built the governance of funds at regional level. In the 2007-2013 perspective, voivodship offices served as certifying bodies, i.e. those that verified what was happening in the programmes, in EU funds at regional level. So that institutional memory is there all the time and there are still people who worked and have experience there. We will actually use them for a fraction of the issues that we, as the Ministry, are obliged to implement under the cohesion policy. And we are hoping that this will make these funds better spent and that from the voivodeship - this is his role - we will have strong support. The question is whether the voivode is needed in this system. The first point that you were talking about is his role in the monitoring committees. Well, first of all, I would like to remind you that at this moment on 13 committees on 16 voivodships have their representatives, even they have a stronger power, because they are active participants, i.e. participate in the decision-making process. The role we want to give them through the proposed changes is to be an observer of what is happening. And that is generally how we want to see the role of voivodship offices, but on the other hand, if they are observers, they are involved in monitoring, supervising our orders as ministries, they should not participate in the decision-making process. We want to avoid a conflict of interest that could occur in this case. We hope that the voivods here have much better knowledge of what is happening at regional level, have institutional memory, have people. But I accept the suggestion which has been addressed to me, namely that it would be worth strengthening the voivodship offices in this role. So that we will undoubtedly consider this suggestion here. Another role of the voivodship is to participate in the project selection process as an observer. To be honest, I don't see anything out of the ordinary, nothing out of the ordinary. I must tell you that while I was still working in the European Commission, I have been involved in hundreds of Committees on Social Affairs. the selection of projects, and it was as an observer, the funds were in Poland, the Poles managed these funds. My role was just an observer, not an active decision-maker. And so we see the role of representatives of the voivodship in the evaluation committees of projects. We believe that this will improve the quality of projects, that this will affect the objective of project selection rules. Another issue raised here is the need to act and the role of the Ombudsman. To be honest, when I listened to all those critical voices that were directed to us, I felt that all of these voices were voices of those who, in some way, stood on the side of the institutions managing these funds, in some way defending the side of the administration and not looking very closely at the side of beneficiaries such as entrepreneurs. And once again, our philosophy in making these changes was exactly the opposite. We want to make changes that will make it easier for our beneficiaries to implement the projects. That is why the spokesman is the exact answer to these expectations. It is such a link between those who use EU funds and us, the administration that manages these funds. We do not plan to create any special institutions, offices, these functions are to be performed by the current employees of the managing authorities. The response from the beneficiaries was very positive, e.g. when we presented this concept at the so-called meeting. d.o.b. the simplifications that we have created and which mainly consists of practitioners, i.e. people who have a lot of experience in the preparation and implementation of EU projects. Another issue was the consultation of our project with local authorities in particular, so I would like to emphasise, first of all, that the work on the changes has lasted about 1 year. For the first time we presented these already proposed solutions to local governments at the turn of November and December of last year, and then twice in February we organized meetings dedicated to local governments, where we presented the proposed changes in the law. This draft law was presented in the same way as the opinions of the joint government and local government committee. In December, in plenary, he received a positive opinion from this body. In May 2017, i.e. this year, the Joint Committee of the Government and the Territorial Government changed its opinion from positive to negative. We presume that the basis for this negative opinion was only the provisions concerning the voivodeship, because to the rest of the solution, the principles for which a positive opinion of the committee had previously been issued have not changed. In addition, the Minister for Development and Finance three times referred to comments made by various local governments, and this happened even after the agreement and consultation process of the draft law had been completed. So that this dialogue, these consultations were really broad, which is not so often the case for proposed legislation in our country. The next point that you have highlighted is the programming guidelines. Let me stress once again: this is a change that is expected and which also had a positive response from the beneficiaries. On the other hand, when it comes to managing authorities, of course, everyone would prefer such guidelines to be, because these are guidelines issued by individual managing authorities. We are totally consciously abandoning them, so that the issue of funds is simply simpler. We do not want to force beneficiaries to read, to apply these specific guidelines, but we leave - and I would like to emphasise - horizontal guidelines that apply to all institutions. The next point I wanted to mention here is the provisions on the use of funds that come back from financial instruments after the end of the financial perspective. This is a very important change and it is intended to maintain financial instruments, financial resources for development purposes, so we absolutely do not direct these funds, for example. back to the state budget. I will repeat once again that there was such a test a few years ago that the money that had already been used in the past would simply be transferred to the state budget to reduce the financial deficit so that this would not be the case, both for national programmes and for regional programmes. We're putting in those records. It is in the law that these financial instruments should work for our country, at national or regional level. Please also note that one of the amendments proposed in the Act is the possibility of creating regional development funds. It is these financial instruments that have, among others. at regional level be used by regional development funds. I would also like to point out that EU regulations clearly point out that funds from the past perspective are being used for similar purposes in the next perspective. I would also like to point out that the voivodship authorities in the case of these funds are not the owners of these funds, but are the owners of these measures, while the provisions introduced give security that these funds will be at the disposal of these institutions, i.e. those funds that come back from regional operational programmes will be at the disposal of regions, voivodships, in the case of national programmes in the hands of the competent institutions which managed these financial instruments. So this is an absolutely very important change, which allows us to use EU funds in a much more developmental way. I would still like to address a few of the points that you have highlighted. Yes, it is true, this is the case in every financial perspective, the prospects that differ, and we as those responsible for this in the administration will be simply criticised, and rightly, if we do not make such changes, because each perspective introduces a new solution, and adjustments must be made at a time when they are necessary. So I am also convinced that if there is another perspective, there will be new rules again, there will probably be a need for these changes again. So that it may be a few months too late, but once again I repeat that many changes that did not require any new legislative solutions, we already introduced last year. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u474	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Joint Commission: the Committee on the Protection of the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, the Committee on Public Finance and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, I present a report on the Government's draft Water Law Act, contained in print No 1679. The draft of the new Water Law implements eight directives: sewage, nitrate, water framework directive, bathing water quality directive, groundwater protection directive against pollution, flood directive, marine environment framework directive and water policy environmental quality standards directive. The Act amends 45 existing laws, starting with the Law of 26 March 1982 on merge and exchange of land and ending with the Act of 16 December Ub. on amending certain laws in order to improve the legal environment of entrepreneurs. The content of the new provisions contained in 13 chapters, in 574 articles on 444 pages, concerns a very large number of actors, regulates a very wide range of cases. After a long, huge number of government contributions, the work in the Sejm after first reading on 25 May this year, after the creation of the subcommittee working on the project, was carried out with the participation of numerous representatives of stakeholders. The work of the subcommittee continued from 7, 9, 13, 14, 22 and from 27 to 30 June, and yesterday the joint committees adopted a report which I have the honour of presenting to the High House today. I would like to thank both the ministers and their colleagues, the staff of the Legislative Office of the Sejm, the secretariats of the committees and the representatives of the social side for working together on the project. Now a few words about the contents of the bill. Section I of the General Rules contains three chapters. Chapter 1 sets out the scope, river basin areas, division into 22 water regions, water catchment areas, the jurisdiction of the authorities. Chapter 2 - Explanations of the legal terms in Article. 16 contains 77 definitions used in the term law. Chapter 3 - Waters and water bodies are defined by the division of waters into surface and groundwater, flowing and standing, transitional, coastal and territorial waters. The use of water is divided into three chapters. Chapter 1 The use of water and water services clarifies the widespread, normal and specific use of water and water services. Water services are use beyond common, specific and normal use, e.g. underground or surface water collection, flooring, storage, retention, treatment, collection and treatment of waste water, their introduction into water or to the ground. Chapter 2 of this heading, Waters used for bathing, in its entirety, is a transfer of the content of Directive 2006/7/EC. Chapter III of the Water Protection Directive contains seven chapters. Chapter 1 sets out, inter alia, the objective of water protection and environmental objectives. in Article 4, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 56 environmental targets for single surface water bodies not designated as artificial or strongly modified. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 57 sets out these objectives for artificial and heavily modified water bodies, in Article. 59 - for groundwater and in Article. 61 - for protected areas. Chapter 2 of the Water Protection Rules provides for prohibitions and rules on the introduction of waste water into waters. Chapter 3 of the Urban Waste Water Cleaning Regulation sets out the manner and rules for the designation of agglomerations and the reporting obligations of municipalities, Polish Waters and the Minister responsible for. water management. Let us stress that Article. 99 provides the basis for the adoption of regulations specifying substances which are particularly harmful to the environment, the conditions to be met when the waste water is introduced into the water, the maximum levels of pollutants, the conditions to be met when the rainwater or watermelon is introduced into the water. In turn, the article. 100 provides the basis for a regulation on substances which are particularly harmful to the aquatic environment, the introduction of which in industrial waste water to sewage installations requires water law. During the debate on the provisions of the Article. 86 and 99 were analyzed the problem of urban waste water treatment in industrial treatment plants, as is the case in Kwidzyn and Świecie. However, this case may be dealt with in the Regulation. Chapter 4 of this chapter is the implementation of the Nitrates Directive. The legislation requires agricultural production, including special agricultural production departments and activities, where animal manure or fertilisers are stored in order to prevent water pollution with nitrates. In order to reduce water pollution with nitrates, the Minister responsible for. Water management in agreement with the Minister responsible for agriculture is developing an action programme. Let us stress that, according to these records, the annual dose of livestock manure used in agriculture should not exceed 170 kg of nitrogen per hectare. However, if this does not jeopardise the achievement of the objectives set out in the Act, it will be possible to apply a higher dose. Chapter 5 of this heading contains the principles for monitoring by the competent authorities of environmental inspections, inter alia. the priority substances referred to in Article. 115. Chapter 6 The protection of water shots and inland reservoirs lays down rules for the establishment, rigors, the determination of direct and indirect zones and of protective areas for water shots and reservoirs. Chapter 7 The protection of the marine environment provides that, in order to protect marine waters, a maritime strategy is to be developed and implemented: preliminary assessment of the environmental status of marine waters, environmental objective for marine waters, monitoring programme and marine water protection programme. This programme develops Polish waters in agreement with ministers: national defence, construction, maritime economy, agriculture, fisheries, environment, home affairs and health. Section IV Flood risk management and prevention of drought. Chapter 1 of this chapter is flood risk management. Let us remind you that this is the task of Polish Waters, government and local government administration. Areas of particular flood risk shall be included in the planning documents. It shall constitute an Article. Article 166 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1, there is this provision. The drafts of these documents are subject to agreement with the competent units of Polish Waters. A preliminary flood risk assessment shall be carried out for river basin areas. Flood risk maps shall be drawn up for areas at risk of flooding identified in this preliminary flood risk assessment. For the areas referred to in Article. Article 4 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2, i.e. flood risk areas and areas exposed to flooding in the event of damage to shafts and flooring structures, flood risk maps shall be drawn up. On the basis of these maps, flood risk management plans are prepared. They are prepared by the Polish Waters with the Minister of Infrastructure and voivodships, and are evaluated by the marshals. The provisions of this chapter also contain bans on works or activities necessary to maintain the stability of flood shafts. Chapter 2 deals with plans to counteract the effects of drought. Department V. Water construction and water melioration. Chapter 1 "Water construction' concerns respect for environmental objectives in the design, use and maintenance of water equipment, as well as the obligations of users of waterborne and inland waterway transport structures. In turn, Chapter 2 of this heading sets out rules for recording melioration, co-financing of melioration costs and fixing of melioration fees. Chapter VI. The estate of the State Treasury. Chapter 1 of this heading The ownership of waters and the obligations of their owners are provisions on ownership rights in relation to public waters - these ownership rights are exercised by the Polish Waters - to inland waters flowing and to groundwater, excluding inland waterways of transport importance, in respect of which these ownership rights are exercised by the Minister for Foreign Affairs. inland waterway transport. Minister for Foreign Affairs maritime affairs exercise ownership rights vis-à-vis territorial waters, marine internal waters. Polish waters may, with the agreement of the Minister of Water Management, entrust to the owners the exercise of ownership rights in relation to the waters in the sound of the sound system at the request of the latter. A similar solution was used in the case of local government units and their associations. In the case of land covered by water within national parks, these parks shall exercise perpetual use rights in accordance with Article. 259. Chapter 2 concerns Polish Waters and defines their status. Let me remind you that they have the status of a State legal person within the meaning of Article. The 9th Public Finance Act. They form this structure of the National Water Management Board with its registered office in Warsaw and regional water management boards in Gdańsk, Gliwice, Cracow, Lublin, Poznań, Rzeszów, Szczecin, Warsaw and Wrocław, as well as lower levels, water catchment management and water surveillance. The regulations define the division of competences between the various organisational units of Polish Vodka and the requirements for persons in management positions. Consultative committees are also set up, advisory and advisory bodies composed of representatives of voivodships, marshals, representatives of the local government, the Commission of the Common Government and the Territorial Self-Government and directors of inland waterway authorities. As regards the status of Polish Waters, one issue needs to be mentioned. During the work of the subcommittee, later in the joint committees, we took note of the reservations of the Legislative Bureau regarding the provisions of the article. 141, Art. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with the examination procedure referred to in paragraph 1 of this Article. 3 and Article 4 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [3] shall apply mutatis mutandis. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down These issues are the establishment of an article. 141, that is to establish a protection area by means of a local law act after agreement or opinion, after agreement with the Polish Waters, which have the status of a state legal person, i.e. they are not a local or government entity, they are simply a defined entity, whose status is defined by the Act on public finances. The same applies to Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance 3, i.e. Whereas this provision provides for a temporary derogation from ad hoc action, also adopted by means of a local act; And art. 327, which I mentioned, concerns the adoption of water maintenance plans, including by means of local law, after agreement with the Polish Waters. In this section, Chapter 3 defines the financial economy of Polish Waters, in turn in Chapter 4 - the management of the property of the State Treasury. Chapter 5 Economic instruments for water management. These instruments are, first, water service charges, increased fees, waterway charges, legalisation fee, annual fee, annual fee for inland fishing. I would like to remind you that an amendment has been adopted in the course of the work of the subcommittee, which allows the payment of annual payments to fishing farmers. High Izbo! Section VII of Water Management sets out the planning, information system for water management, water management control and water monitoring. Chapter VIII Water power. Chapter 1 defines the competence of the Minister responsible for water management, competence and responsibilities for water surveillance. The latter, water law assessments, which are a novelty, will be issued for investments or activities likely to affect the ability to achieve the environmental objectives referred to. I was just talking about art here. 56, 57, 59 and 61, i.e. setting environmental targets for different types of water. Department X of water companies and shaft compounds. They also require the notification to be entered in the information system of water management conducted by the Polish Waters. Section XI, "Indemnity', of course, concerns, as the name indicates, liability for damages, but that compensation is granted, in the shortest sense, for damages, except for those arising in the fight against major accidents, the need to ensure floodwater flows, the need to ensure the integrity and stability of flood shafts, the need to lower the level of flooring and to prohibit movement in flood shafts. Section XIII, Criminal provisions, contains criminal provisions in seven articles. There aren't many of them, so you can read them. I am not going to specify and quote exactly these provisions. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-05-sejm-45-1.u544	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The year 2017 is the year of the 800th anniversary of Opole. On this occasion in our city there are many different events and events organized by the city and institutions. June 24th this year The main ecclesiastical celebrations related to this anniversary were held, an act of solemn commitment to Our Lady of Opole. This ceremony was preceded by a peregrination of a copy of the painting of Our Lady of Opole, which since 2 February this year. She visited Opole parishes. The painting of Our Lady of Opole was created at the end of the 15th century in Moravia. It was painted by an unknown artist on three connected lip boards. Wonderful image was worshiped first in Silesian Piekary and since 1702 in Opole. A copy of it was left in Silesian bakeries. The Opole Madonna was always surrounded by a great honor of the faithful. Before the miraculous painting he prayed, among others. King John III Sobieski before Vienna's rescue, King August II Strong, Fr. Archbishop Achilles Ratti, later Pope Pius XI. 21 June 1983 during his second pilgrimage to his homeland Pope John Paul II on the Mount St. Anna crowned the painting with papal silver-golden crowns. The climax of the church celebrations of the 800th anniversary of the city was the procession that set out from the church of St. Our Lady of Pain and St. Wojciech on Górka with a copy of the painting, which he previously peregrinated after the Opole parishes. The residents of Opole, together with the representatives of the church authorities, with the bishop and the state and local authorities with the president of Opole, crossed the streets of the city. The purpose of the procession was the Opole Cathedral, where the Bishop of Opole, Prof. Andrzej Czaja, made an historical dedication of the city to Our Lady of Opole. The parish priest of the cathedral parish Fr. Dr. Waldemar Klinger said earlier, and I quote: the act of entrusting the city to Our Lady is part of the history of Catholic piety. This will be a benchmark for future generations. In the act of trust, the Bishop of Opole spoke, among others.: Mrs. Opolska, we give you the inhabitants of our city, all together and individually. We entrust you, Mother, to those who are responsible for social well-being, well-being and security by virtue of these offices. We entrust you, Mother, marriages and families, teachers, educators and catechists, children and young people. The idea of confiding in the city is to make modern citizens aware that 800 years of Opole's history are 800 years of Christianity and the Catholic faith that shaped this city. Thank you for your attention.
